}\n225\t\n226\t            return abortReport;\n227\t        }\n228\t\n229\t        RunReport report = BuildReport(plan, settled, cancelled: cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested);\n230\t\n231\t        // --- C1 terminal whole-repo integration gate (§3.3/§4a) --------------------------\n232\t        // After every task settles green, re-run the run's integration-guardrail set on the\n233\t        // FINAL plan-branch HEAD (via the integration worktree) — the union's whole-repo\n234\t        // soundness boundary that backstops the per-hop FF-integrations a linear chain skipped.\n235\t        // A failing gate flips the run off-green (the sink task, or first task, to needs-human)\n236\t        // so mergeOnSuccess is refused and the report is not certified.\n237\t        //\n238\t        // preflights-impl deliverable 4: REPLACED by the terminal PlanGuardrailPhase (Guardrails.Cli,\n239\t        // SSOT §3.3/§7.1) for any plan that declares a plan-level <plan>/guardrails/ folder — that\n240\t        // phase runs AFTER this method returns, against the SAME merged HEAD, via the same IReVerifier\n241\t        // seam. This legacy per-task integrationGate/scope:\"integration\" sink-task run is SUPERSEDED\n242\t        // (never both) whenever plan.PlanGuardrails is non-empty; it stays live, unchanged, only for a\n243\t        // plan still on the retired sink-task modelling.\n244\t        if (report.AllSucceeded && _reVerifier != null && integ != null && plan.PlanGuardrails.Count == 0)\n245\t        {\n246\t            IReadOnlyList<GuardrailDefinition> integrationSet =\n247\t                GuardrailScopeFilter.IntegrationSet(plan.Tasks.SelectMany(t => t.Guardrails));\n248\t\n249\t            if (integrationSet.Count > 0)\n250\t            {\n251\t                ReVerifyResult gate = await _reVerifier\n252\t                    .ReVerifyAsync(integ.IntegrationWorktreePath, integrationSet, cancellationToken)\n253\t                    .ConfigureAwait(false);\n254\t\n255\t                if (!gate.Passed)\n256\t                {\n257\t                    report = WithTerminalGateFailure(plan, report, gate);\n258\t                }\n259\t            }\n260\t        }\n261\t\n262\t        // Deliver the completed plan branch to the user's branch when every task succeeded\n263\t        // and mergeOnSuccess is enabled. AI-merge is withheld: a conflict halts to needs-human\n264\t        // with the plan branch intact (SSOT §5.3).\n265\t        MergeOnSuccessResult? mergeOutcome = null;\n266\t        string? mergeDetail = null;\n267\t        if (report.AllSucceeded && plan.Config.MergeOnSuccess && _worktreeProvider != null && integ != null)\n268\t        {\n269\t            mergeOutcome = _worktreeProvider.MergePlanBranchIntoUserBranch(integ, cancellationToken);\n270\t            // Issue #150: when the user's git hook rejected the user-facing merge, surface the hook's\n271\t            // stderr so the CLI can show the actual reason (and a remedy) instead of a bare outcome.\n272\t            if (mergeOutcome == MergeOnSuccessResult.HookRejected)\n273\t            {\n274\t                mergeDetail = _worktreeProvider.LastMergeOnSuccessDetail;\n275\t            }\n276\t        }\n277\t\n278\t        // --- M2 end-of-run cleanup sweep (closes #126) -----------------------------------\n279\t        // After delivery, Discard every still-live segment/fork worktree directory this run created\n280\t        // (whatever remains owned in DirectoryOwner — the failure path already removed any it freed),\n281\t        // then PruneOrphans to clear the registrations. The integration worktree is reattached to the\n282\t        // plan branch and is NEVER in DirectoryOwner, so the sweep never touches it (design §D / 08 §7).\n283\t        //\n284\t        // Skipped on cancellation: a cancelled run journals in-flight tasks back to pending for\n285\t        // resume, and Discarding here would race the executor's in-flight teardown (design §C). The\n286\t        // next run's PruneStaleRunBranches pre-pass reclaims those directories.\n287\t        if (!cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested)\n288\t        {\n289\t            EndOfRunSweep(context, integ);\n290\t        }\n291\t\n292\t        return report with { MergeOnSuccessOutcome = mergeOutcome, MergeOnSuccessDetail = mergeDetail };\n293\t    }\n294\t\n295\t    /// <summary>\n296\t    /// Build the <see cref=\"RunAbort\"/> for an infrastructure fault (issue #150): a one-line headline\n297\t    /// + remedy for the console, and the full exception text for the run logs. A dev tool keeps the\n298\t    /// detail — just not as the headline.\n299\t    /// </summary>\n300\t    private static RunAbort BuildAbort(Exception fault) => new()\n301\t    {\n302\t        Headline = $\"The run was aborted by an unexpected infrastructure fault: {fault.Message}\",\n303\t        Remedy = \"See the full exception in the run logs below. This is a harness/environment fault \"\n304\t               + \"(e.g. an offline or failing git hook on an internal commit, or git unavailable), \"\n305\t               + \"not a task failure — resolve it and re-run to resume.\",\n306\t        Detail = fault.ToString()\n307\t    };\n308\t\n309\t    /// <summary>\n310\t    /// plan 08 topology-wiring M2 §D (#126): remove every segment/fork worktree directory owned by a\n311\t    /// task that settled GREEN, then prune stale registrations. A green task's work is durable on the\n312\t    /// plan branch, so its directory is pure waste — this is the direct #126 fix (a wholly-green run\n313\t    /// leaves no segment worktree behind).\n314\t    ///\n315\t    /// A NON-green task (needs-human / failed / blocked) keeps its directory: the \"fix, don't restart\"\n316\t    /// invariant (§3.2, open-risk #4) requires a failed attempt's worktree to survive so a human — or\n317\t    /// a resume's reset-and-retry — can inspect the scoped-revert artifacts and WIP. The next run's\n318\t    /// PruneStaleRunBranches pre-pass reclaims those. The <c>_integration</c> worktree is never in\n319\t    /// <see cref=\"RunContext.DirectoryOwner\"/> and is therefore never swept.\n320\t    ///\n321\t    /// Best-effort — a cleanup failure logs (via <see cref=\"IRunObserver.CleanupFailed\"/>) and\n322\t    /// continues; it must NEVER flip an otherwise-green run off-green (GitWorktreeProvider.Discard\n323\t    /// throws on a non-zero git exit, so each call-site swallows).\n324\t    /// </summary>\n325\t    private void EndOfRunSweep(RunContext context, IntegrationHandle? integ)\n326\t    {\n327\t        if (_worktreeProvider is not { } provider || integ is null)\n328\t        {\n329\t            return;\n330\t        }\n331\t\n332\t        // Snapshot under the gate (no workers are running now, but keep the discipline consistent).\n333\t        // Sweep only GREEN-owned directories; non-green tasks keep their worktree for fix/resume.\n334\t        List<KeyValuePair<string, string>> sweepable;\n335\t        lock (_gate)\n336\t        {\n337\t            sweepable = context.DirectoryOwner\n338\t                .Where(kv => context.Settled.TryGetValue(kv.Value, out TaskResult? r) && r.IsGreen)\n339\t                .ToList();\n340\t        }\n341\t\n342\t        foreach ((string path, string owner) in sweepable)\n343\t        {\n344\t            try\n345\t            {\n346\t                provider.Discard(new WorktreeHandle { WorktreePath = path });\n347\t            }\n348\t            catch (Exception ex)\n349\t            {\n350\t                _observer.CleanupFailed(owner, ex);\n351\t            }\n352\t            finally\n353\t            {\n354\t                lock (_gate)\n355\t                {\n356\t                    context.DirectoryOwner.Remove(path);\n357\t                }\n358\t            }\n359\t        }\n360\t\n361\t        try\n362\t        {\n363\t            provider.PruneOrphans(Array.Empty<string>(), integ);\n364\t        }\n365\t        catch (Exception ex)\n366\t        {\n367\t            _observer.CleanupFailed(\"(prune-orphans)\", ex);\n368\t        }\n369\t    }\n370\t\n371\t    private async Task WorkerLoopAsync(RunContext context, CancellationTokenSource runCts)\n372\t    {\n373\t        CancellationToken cancellationToken = runCts.Token;\n374\t        try\n375\t        {\n376\t            await foreach (TaskEnvelope envelope in context.Channel.Reader.ReadAllAsync(cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false))\n377\t            {\n378\t                TaskNode task = envelope.Task;\n379\t                WorktreeHandle handle = MaterializeForkIfDeferred(context, envelope);\n380\t\n381\t                if (CostCapHaltFor(task) is { } capped)\n382\t                {\n383\t                    await OnSettledAsync(context, task, capped, handle, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n384\t                    continue;\n385\t                }\n386\t\n387\t                TaskResult result = await _executor.ExecuteAsync(task, handle, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n388\t\n389\t                await OnSettledAsync(context, task, result, handle, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n390\t            }\n391\t        }\n392\t        catch (OperationCanceledException)\n393\t        {\n394\t            // Cancelled drain.\n395\t        }\n396\t        catch (Exception ex)\n397\t        {\n398\t            lock (_gate)\n399\t            {\n400\t                _fault ??= ex;\n401\t            }\n402\t\n403\t            context.Channel.Writer.TryComplete();\n404\t            runCts.Cancel();\n405\t        }\n406\t    }\n407\t\n408\t    /// <summary>\n409\t    /// plan 08 topology-wiring M1 §B: materialize a deferred fork-the-rest sibling's worktree at\n410\t    /// dequeue — the actual <c>git worktree add</c> runs HERE, OFF the <see cref=\"_gate\"/> every\n411\t    /// settling worker contends for. The fork roots off the producer's RECORDED sha (captured in\n412\t    /// the request under <c>_gate</c> at assignment, W-2), never a live rev-parse of the segment\n413\t    /// branch the inheritor may have advanced. Returns the envelope's existing handle unchanged\n414\t    /// when there is no deferred fork.\n415\t    /// </summary>\n416\t    private WorktreeHandle MaterializeForkIfDeferred(RunContext context, TaskEnvelope envelope)\n417\t    {\n418\t        if (envelope.Fork is not { } fork || _worktreeProvider is not { } provider)\n419\t        {\n420\t            return envelope.Handle;\n421\t        }\n422\t\n423\t        // git I/O off the gate.\n424\t        WorktreeHandle handle = provider.ForkFromTip(fork.ProducerRecordedSha, envelope.Task.Id, attempt: 1);\n425\t\n426\t        // Bookkeeping under the gate: record the assigned handle + directory ownership.\n427\t        lock (_gate)\n428\t        {\n429\t            context.Handles[envelope.Task.Id] = handle;\n430\t            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(handle.WorktreePath))\n431\t            {\n432\t                context.DirectoryOwner[handle.WorktreePath] = envelope.Task.Id;\n433\t            }\n434\t        }\n435\t\n436\t        return handle;\n437\t    }\n438\t\n439\t    private TaskResult? CostCapHaltFor(TaskNode task)\n440\t    {\n441\t        if (_plan.Config.MaxCostUsd is not { } cap || _journal.CurrentCostUsd() < cap)\n442\t        {\n443\t            return null;\n444\t        }\n445\t\n446\t        return new TaskResult\n447\t        {\n448\t            TaskId = task.Id,\n449\t            Outcome = TaskOutcome.NeedsHuman,\n450\t            Summary = $\"cost cap reached: cumulative journaled cost has reached the configured \" +\n451\t                      $\"maxCostUsd (${cap}); task not launched.\"\n452\t        };\n453\t    }\n454\t\n455\t    /// <summary>\n456\t    /// Called after a task finishes (executor or cost-cap halt). For worktree-mode green results,\n457\t    /// performs the B1 deferred settle (fragment merge → git integration commit → journal settle)\n458\t    /// under <see cref=\"_integrationLock\"/> BEFORE updating the shared run context under\n459\t    /// <see cref=\"_gate\"/>. This ordering ensures dependents only become ready after the upstream\n460\t    /// integration has advanced the plan branch, making lazy handle creation FF-compatible.\n461\t    /// </summary>\n462\t    private async Task OnSettledAsync(\n463\t        RunContext context, TaskNode task, TaskResult result, WorktreeHandle handle, CancellationToken ct)\n464\t    {\n465\t        // B1 deferred settle (worktree mode, real segment): ValidateFragmentForSettle sets\n466\t        // DeferredSettle=true, meaning the Scheduler owns the fragment merge → git commit →\n467\t        // journal settle sequence under the integration lock.\n468\t        //\n469\t        // Old path (serial mode or fake provider): the executor already merged + journaled;\n470\t        // just call provider.Integrate directly so IWorktreeProvider.IntegrateCallCount tests pass.\n471\t        if (result.IsGreen && _worktreeProvider is { } provider && context.Integ is { } integ)\n472\t        {\n473\t            if (result.DeferredSettle)\n474\t            {\n475\t                await _integrationLock.WaitAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n476\t                try\n477\t                {\n478\t                    result = await SettleAsync(task, result, handle, provider, integ, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n479\t                }\n480\t                finally\n481\t                {\n482\t                    _integrationLock.Release();\n483\t                }\n484\t            }\n485\t            else if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(handle.WorktreePath))\n486\t            {\n487\t                // Non-deferred: executor already handled journal; just integrate the segment.\n488\t                provider.Integrate(handle, integ, CancellationToken.None);\n489\t            }\n490\t\n491\t            // #195 retry-salvage pruning (deliverable 6): once a task's FINAL settle (after the\n492\t            // deferred B1 settle above, which can still turn a green-looking result into NeedsHuman on\n493\t            // a failed union re-verify) is truly Succeeded, its salvage refs — its own prior\n494\t            // rolled-back partial attempts — have served their purpose and are pruned so they never\n495\t            // accumulate across a long-lived repo. Checked on result.Outcome (the POST-settle value),\n496\t            // never result.IsGreen (which is true before SettleAsync can still flip it to NeedsHuman).\n497\t            if (result.Outcome == TaskOutcome.Succeeded)\n498\t            {\n499\t                try { provider.PruneSalvageRefs(task.Id); }\n500\t                catch (Exception ex) { _observer.CleanupFailed(task.Id, ex); }\n501\t            }\n502\t        }\n503\t\n504\t        var newlyReady = new List<TaskEnvelope>();\n505\t        var newlyBlocked = new List<TaskResult>();\n506\t\n507\t        lock (_gate)\n508\t        {\n509\t            context.Settled[task.Id] = result;\n510\t            context.Remaining--;\n511\t\n512\t            if (result.IsGreen)\n513\t            {\n514\t                // Which dependents of this producer just had their LAST pending dep cleared?\n515\t                // (A multi-producer dependent here is a fan-in: it has other, already-green\n516\t                // producers, and reaches the merged plan tip — never reused, M1 §A1.)\n517\t                var justReady = new List<string>();\n518\t                foreach (string dependent in context.Graph.DependentsOf(task.Id))\n519\t                {\n520\t                    if (!context.Settled.ContainsKey(dependent) && --context.PendingDeps[dependent] == 0)\n521\t                    {\n522\t                        justReady.Add(dependent);\n523\t                    }\n524\t                }\n525\t\n526\t                AssignDependentHandles(context, task, justReady, newlyReady);\n527\t            }\n528\t            else if (result.Outcome != TaskOutcome.Cancelled)\n529\t            {\n530\t                foreach (string dependent in context.Graph.TransitiveDependentsOf(task.Id)\n531\t                             .OrderBy(d => d, StringComparer.Ordinal))\n532\t                {\n533\t                    if (context.Settled.ContainsKey(dependent))\n534\t                    {\n535\t                        continue;\n536\t                    }\n537\t\n538\t                    var blocked = new TaskResult\n539\t                    {\n540\t                        TaskId = dependent,\n541\t                        Outcome = TaskOutcome.Blocked,\n542\t                        Summary = $\"blocked: dependency '{task.Id}' did not succeed\"\n543\t                    };\n544\t                    context.Settled[dependent] = blocked;\n545\t                    context.Remaining--;\n546\t                    _journal.MarkBlocked(dependent);\n547\t                    newlyBlocked.Add(blocked);\n548\t                }\n549\t\n550\t                // M2 §C / open-risk #4: a permanently-failed (needs-human/failed) task's segment is\n551\t                // NOT Discarded mid-run. The \"fix, don't restart\" invariant (§3.2) keeps a failed\n552\t                // attempt's worktree alive so a human (or a retry) can inspect the scoped-revert\n553\t                // artifacts and WIP. Its directory stays owned in DirectoryOwner and is reclaimed by\n554\t                // the end-of-run sweep at quiescence — which is what closes #126 (design §D point 2:\n555\t                // \"#126 is closed by the run-end sweep alone\"). Cancellation skips the sweep, so a\n556\t                // cancelled task's worktree survives for the resume prune (T-11).\n557\t            }\n558\t\n559\t            if (context.Remaining == 0)\n560\t            {\n561\t                context.Channel.Writer.TryComplete();\n562\t            }\n563\t        }\n564\t\n565\t        _observer.TaskFinished(result);\n566\t        foreach (TaskResult blocked in newlyBlocked)\n567\t        {\n568\t            _observer.TaskFinished(blocked);\n569\t        }\n570\t\n571\t        // Each envelope already carries its assigned handle (fresh segment / reused directory) OR a\n572\t        // deferred fork request the worker materializes off-gate at dequeue (M1 §B).\n573\t        foreach (TaskEnvelope ready in newlyReady)\n574\t        {\n575\t            context.Channel.Writer.TryWrite(ready);\n576\t        }\n577\t    }\n578\t\n579\t    /// <summary>\n580\t    /// plan 08 topology-wiring M1 §A/§B: assign worktree handles to the dependents of a just-settled\n581\t    /// green producer <paramref name=\"producer\"/>, choosing reuse vs fork vs fresh-segment.\n582\t    /// <list type=\"bullet\">\n583\t    ///   <item><b>Multi-producer dependents (fan-in)</b> get a fresh <see cref=\"IWorktreeProvider.CreateSegment\"/>\n584\t    ///     off the plan-branch tip, which already contains every producer's integrated work — never\n585\t    ///     reused (§A1).</item>\n586\t    ///   <item><b>Single-producer dependents</b> are the inherit-one/fork-rest fan-out. The inheritor\n587\t    ///     (longest downstream chain via <see cref=\"DependencyGraph.TransitiveDependentsOf\"/>, ordinal-id\n588\t    ///     tiebreak) reuses the producer's segment directory via the pure-handle\n589\t    ///     <see cref=\"IWorktreeProvider.ReuseSegment\"/> (safe under <see cref=\"_gate\"/>; ownership\n590\t    ///     transfers to the inheritor). The rest fork off the producer's RECORDED sha — a DEFERRED\n591\t    ///     request the worker materializes off-gate (§B, W-2).</item>\n592\t    /// </list>\n593\t    /// Runs under <see cref=\"_gate\"/>. All assignment + bookkeeping is here; only the fork's\n594\t    /// <c>git worktree add</c> is deferred off-gate.\n595\t    /// </summary>\n596\t    private void AssignDependentHandles(\n597\t        RunContext context, TaskNode producer, List<string> justReady, List<TaskEnvelope> newlyReady)\n598\t    {\n599\t        // Fan-in (multi-producer) dependents reach the merged plan tip with a fresh segment.\n600\t        var singleProducer = new List<string>();\n601\t        foreach (string dependent in justReady)\n602\t        {\n603\t            if (context.ById[dependent].DependsOn.Count > 1)\n604\t            {\n605\t                WorktreeHandle fanInHandle = CreateFreshSegment(context, dependent);\n606\t                context.Handles[dependent] = fanInHandle;\n607\t                RecordOwnership(context, fanInHandle, dependent);\n608\t                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], fanInHandle));\n609\t            }\n610\t            else\n611\t            {\n612\t                singleProducer.Add(dependent);\n613\t            }\n614\t        }\n615\t\n616\t        if (singleProducer.Count == 0)\n617\t        {\n618\t            return;\n619\t        }\n620\t\n621\t        // Inherit-one: the single-producer dependent with the longest downstream chain reuses the\n622\t        // producer's directory; ordinal-id tiebreak. The producer's handle carries the RecordedSha\n623\t        // that Integrate captured during this settle (strict happens-before).\n624\t        string inheritor = singleProducer\n625\t            .OrderByDescending(d => context.Graph.TransitiveDependentsOf(d).Count)\n626\t            .ThenBy(d => d, StringComparer.Ordinal)\n627\t            .First();\n628\t\n629\t        WorktreeHandle? producerHandle =\n630\t            _worktreeProvider != null ? context.Handles.GetValueOrDefault(producer.Id) : null;\n631\t\n632\t        foreach (string dependent in singleProducer)\n633\t        {\n634\t            if (dependent == inheritor && _worktreeProvider is { } reuseProvider && producerHandle is { } ph)\n635\t            {\n636\t                // Pure handle rewrite — no git, safe under _gate. Ownership of the producer's\n637\t                // directory transfers to the inheritor.\n638\t                WorktreeHandle reused = reuseProvider.ReuseSegment(ph, dependent, attempt: 1);\n639\t                context.Handles[dependent] = reused;\n640\t                if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(reused.WorktreePath))\n641\t                {\n642\t                    context.DirectoryOwner[reused.WorktreePath] = dependent;\n643\t                }\n644\t                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], reused));\n645\t            }\n646\t            else if (_worktreeProvider is not null && producerHandle is { } pf)\n647\t            {\n648\t                // Fork-the-rest: defer the git worktree add to the worker (off-gate). Root off the\n649\t                // producer's RECORDED sha — never the live segment-branch tip the inheritor advances.\n650\t                var fork = new ForkRequest(pf.RecordedCommitSha);\n651\t                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], new WorktreeHandle(), fork));\n652\t            }\n653\t            else\n654\t            {\n655\t                // No provider (serial/fake-less mode): an empty placeholder handle, as before.\n656\t                var placeholder = new WorktreeHandle();\n657\t                context.Handles[dependent] = placeholder;\n658\t                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], placeholder));\n659\t            }\n660\t        }\n661\t    }\n662\t\n663\t    /// <summary>Create a fresh segment off the plan-branch tip (or an empty handle without a provider).</summary>\n664\t    private WorktreeHandle CreateFreshSegment(RunContext context, string taskId) =>\n665\t        _worktreeProvider != null && context.Integ != null\n666\t            ? _worktreeProvider.CreateSegment(taskId, attempt: 1, context.Integ, CancellationToken.None)\n667\t            : new WorktreeHandle();\n668\t\n669\t    /// <summary>Record directory ownership for a non-empty handle path (M0 bookkeeping; under <see cref=\"_gate\"/>).</summary>\n670\t    private static void RecordOwnership(RunContext context, WorktreeHandle handle, string taskId)\n671\t    {\n672\t        if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(handle.WorktreePath))\n673\t        {\n674\t            context.DirectoryOwner[handle.WorktreePath] = taskId;\n675\t        }\n676\t    }\n677\t\n678\t    /// <summary>\n679\t    /// B1 fixed-order settle under <see cref=\"_integrationLock\"/>:\n680\t    /// (1) deep-merge fragment into state.json,\n681\t    /// (2) git integration commit (FF or non-FF merge),\n682\t    /// (3) reserve mergeSequence + journal RecordSettle.\n683\t    /// On non-FF failure: restore state.json, reset integration worktree, journal NeedsHuman.\n684\t    /// </summary>\n685\t    private async Task<TaskResult> SettleAsync(\n686\t        TaskNode task,\n687\t        TaskResult result,\n688\t        WorktreeHandle handle,\n689\t        IWorktreeProvider provider,\n690\t        IntegrationHandle integ,\n691\t        CancellationToken ct)\n692\t    {\n693\t        string statePath = Path.Combine(_plan.PlanDirectory, \"state\", \"state.json\");\n694\t        string preMergeState = File.Exists(statePath) ? File.ReadAllText(statePath) : \"{}\";\n695\t\n696\t        // B1 step 1: merge fragment into state.json BEFORE the git commit.\n697\t        if (result.FragmentPath is { } fp && File.Exists(fp))\n698\t        {\n699\t            MergeFragmentIntoState(statePath, preMergeState, fp);\n700\t        }\n701\t\n702\t        // B1 step 2: git integration commit (FF or non-FF union).\n703\t        IntegrationResult integResult = provider.Integrate(handle, integ, ct);\n704\t\n705\t        if (integResult == IntegrationResult.FastForward)\n706\t        {\n707\t            // FF is free — no re-verify needed. Consume one merge sequence.\n708\t            long seq = _journal.ReserveMergeSequence();\n709\t            RecordSucceededSettle(task, result, seq);\n710\t            return result;\n711\t        }\n712\t\n713\t        if (integResult == IntegrationResult.Conflict)\n714\t        {\n715\t            // AI merge worker resolves the conflict (§9.1). // --- resume pre-pass: journaled successes + plan-branch trailers are green ----------\n        var preSettledGreen = new HashSet<string>(StringComparer.Ordinal);\n        foreach (TaskNode task in plan.Tasks)\n        {\n            if (_journal.StatusOf(task.Id) == JournalTaskStatus.Succeeded\n                || planBranchSettled.Contains(task.Id))\n            {\n                preSettledGreen.Add(task.Id);\n                var skipped = new TaskResult\n                {\n                    TaskId = task.Id,\n                    Outcome = TaskOutcome.Skipped,\n                    Summary = \"already succeeded (resumed) — skipped\"\n                };\n                settled[task.Id] = skipped;\n                _observer.TaskFinished(skipped);\n            }\n        }\n\n        int remaining = 0;\n        foreach (TaskNode task in plan.Tasks)\n        {\n            if (preSettledGreen.Contains(task.Id))\n            {\n                continue;\n            }\n\n            remaining++;\n            pendingDeps[task.Id] = task.DependsOn.Count(d => !preSettledGreen.Contains(d));\n        }\n\n        if (remaining == 0)\n        {\n            return BuildReport(plan, settled, cancelled: false);\n        }\n\n        // Pre-create worktree handles only for initially-ready tasks (no pending deps).\n        // Dependent tasks get their handles created LAZILY in OnSettledAsync AFTER the upstream\n        // integration commits advance the plan branch — this is what makes FF possible for\n        // linear chains: task B's segment is forked from task A's integrated HEAD, not from the\n        // original plan-branch HEAD before A ran.\n        var handles = new Dictionary<string, WorktreeHandle>(StringComparer.Ordinal);\n        // DirectoryOwner (plan 08 topology-wiring M0): worktree path → current owning task id.\n        // The single source of truth for \"is this directory free to Discard / reuse?\". Populated\n        // on every CreateSegment/ForkFromTip; ownership transfers on ReuseSegment (M1). In M0 every\n        // task still owns its own fresh directory, so this map mirrors the fresh-per-task baseline.\n        var directoryOwner = new Dictionary<string, string>(StringComparer.Ordinal);\n        foreach (TaskNode task in plan.Tasks)\n        {\n            if (!preSettledGreen.Contains(task.Id) && pendingDeps[task.Id] == 0)\n            {\n                WorktreeHandle handle = _worktreeProvider != null && integ != null\n                    ? _worktreeProvider.CreateSegment(task.Id, attempt: 1, integ, cancellationToken)\n                    : new WorktreeHandle();\n                handles[task.Id] = handle;\n                if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(handle.WorktreePath))\n                {\n                    directoryOwner[handle.WorktreePath] = task.Id;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        foreach (TaskNode task in plan.Tasks)\n        {\n            if (!preSettledGreen.Contains(task.Id) && pendingDeps[task.Id] == 0)\n            {\n                channel.Writer.TryWrite(new TaskEnvelope(task, handles[task.Id]));\n            }\n        }\n\n        // --- workers ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n        using var runCts = CancellationTokenSource.CreateLinkedTokenSource(cancellationToken);\n        var context = new RunContext(graph, byId, settled, pendingDeps, channel, remaining, handles, directoryOwner, integ);\n        int workerCount = Math.Min(_maxParallelism, remaining);\n        Task[] workers = Enumerable.Range(0, workerCount)\n            .Select(_ => Task.Run(() => WorkerLoopAsync(context, runCts), CancellationToken.None))\n            .ToArray();\n\n        await Task.WhenAll(workers).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n        // Issue #150 — honest halt instead of an unhandled crash. An unexpected infrastructure fault\n        // (a task executor or integration step threw — e.g. an offline GitGuardian pre-commit hook\n        // failing an INTERNAL commit, or git being unavailable) used to RE-THROW here, escaping\n        // unhandled to the CLI as a raw stack-trace headline. Instead, build an ABORTED report that\n        // carries the fault reason, still run the end-of-run cleanup sweep below, and let the CLI\n        // render a one-line diagnostic + remedy (full detail to the logs) and exit non-zero.\n        if (_fault is { } fault)\n        {\n            var abortReport = BuildReport(plan, settled, cancelled: cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested)\n                with { Abort = BuildAbort(fault) };\n\n            // Clean up this run's still-live worktrees exactly as the success path does, so an aborted\n            // run does not orphan more than the next run's prune pre-pass already reclaims (the\n            // intentional \"fix, don't restart\" retention of non-green segments still holds).\n            if (!cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested)\n            {\n                EndOfRunSweep(context, integ);\n            }\n\n            return abortReport;\n        }\n\n        RunReport report = BuildReport(plan, settled, cancelled: cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested);\n\n        // --- C1 terminal whole-repo integration gate (§3.3/§4a) --------------------------\n        // After every task settles green, re-run the run's integration-guardrail set on the\n        // FINAL plan-branch HEAD (via the integration worktree) — the union's whole-repo\n        // soundness boundary that backstops the per-hop FF-integrations a linear chain skipped.\n        // A failing gate flips the run off-green (the sink task, or first task, to needs-human)\n        // so mergeOnSuccess is refused and the report is not certified.\n        //\n        // preflights-impl deliverable 4: REPLACED by the terminal PlanGuardrailPhase (Guardrails.Cli,\n        // SSOT §3.3/§7.1) for any plan that declares a plan-level <plan>/guardrails/ folder — that\n        // phase runs AFTER this method returns, against the SAME merged HEAD, via the same IReVerifier\n        // seam. This legacy per-task integrationGate/scope:\"integration\" sink-task run is SUPERSEDED\n        // (never both) whenever plan.PlanGuardrails is non-empty; it stays live, unchanged, only for a\n        // plan still on the retired sink-task modelling.\n        if (report.AllSucceeded && _reVerifier != null && integ != null && plan.PlanGuardrails.Count == 0)\n        {\n            IReadOnlyList<GuardrailDefinition> integrationSet =\n                GuardrailScopeFilter.IntegrationSet(plan.Tasks.SelectMany(t => t.Guardrails));\n\n            if (integrationSet.Count > 0)\n            {\n                ReVerifyResult gate = await _reVerifier\n                    .ReVerifyAsync(integ.IntegrationWorktreePath, integrationSet, cancellationToken)\n                    .ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                if (!gate.Passed)\n                {\n                    report = WithTerminalGateFailure(plan, report, gate);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Deliver the completed plan branch to the user's branch when every task succeeded\n        // and mergeOnSuccess is enabled. AI-merge is withheld: a conflict halts to needs-human\n        // with the plan branch intact (SSOT §5.3).\n        MergeOnSuccessResult? mergeOutcome = null;\n        string? mergeDetail = null;\n        if (report.AllSucceeded && plan.Config.MergeOnSuccess && _worktreeProvider != null && integ != null)\n        {\n            mergeOutcome = _worktreeProvider.MergePlanBranchIntoUserBranch(integ, cancellationToken);\n            // Issue #150: when the user's git hook rejected the user-facing merge, surface the hook's\n            // stderr so the CLI can show the actual reason (and a remedy) instead of a bare outcome.\n            if (mergeOutcome == MergeOnSuccessResult.HookRejected)\n            {\n                mergeDetail = _worktreeProvider.LastMergeOnSuccessDetail;\n            }\n        }\n\n        // --- M2 end-of-run cleanup sweep (closes #126) -----------------------------------\n        // After delivery, Discard every still-live segment/fork worktree directory this run created\n        // (whatever remains owned in DirectoryOwner — the failure path already removed any it freed),\n        // then PruneOrphans to clear the registrations. The integration worktree is reattached to the\n        // plan branch and is NEVER in DirectoryOwner, so the sweep never touches it (design §D / 08 §7).\n        //\n        // Skipped on cancellation: a cancelled run journals in-flight tasks back to pending for\n        // resume, and Discarding here would race the executor's in-flight teardown (design §C). The\n        // next run's PruneStaleRunBranches pre-pass reclaims those directories.\n        if (!cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested)\n        {\n            EndOfRunSweep(context, integ);\n        }\n\n        return report with { MergeOnSuccessOutcome = mergeOutcome, MergeOnSuccessDetail = mergeDetail };\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Build the <see cref=\"RunAbort\"/> for an infrastructure fault (issue #150): a one-line headline\n    /// + remedy for the console, and the full exception text for the run logs. A dev tool keeps the\n    /// detail — just not as the headline.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static RunAbort BuildAbort(Exception fault) => new()\n    {\n        Headline = $\"The run was aborted by an unexpected infrastructure fault: {fault.Message}\",\n        Remedy = \"See the full exception in the run logs below. This is a harness/environment fault \"\n               + \"(e.g. an offline or failing git hook on an internal commit, or git unavailable), \"\n               + \"not a task failure — resolve it and re-run to resume.\",\n        Detail = fault.ToString()\n    };\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// plan 08 topology-wiring M2 §D (#126): remove every segment/fork worktree directory owned by a\n    /// task that settled GREEN, then prune stale registrations. A green task's work is durable on the\n    /// plan branch, so its directory is pure waste — this is the direct #126 fix (a wholly-green run\n    /// leaves no segment worktree behind).\n    ///\n    /// A NON-green task (needs-human / failed / blocked) keeps its directory: the \"fix, don't restart\"\n    /// invariant (§3.2, open-risk #4) requires a failed attempt's worktree to survive so a human — or\n    /// a resume's reset-and-retry — can inspect the scoped-revert artifacts and WIP. The next run's\n    /// PruneStaleRunBranches pre-pass reclaims those. The <c>_integration</c> worktree is never in\n    /// <see cref=\"RunContext.DirectoryOwner\"/> and is therefore never swept.\n    ///\n    /// Best-effort — a cleanup failure logs (via <see cref=\"IRunObserver.CleanupFailed\"/>) and\n    /// continues; it must NEVER flip an otherwise-green run off-green (GitWorktreeProvider.Discard\n    /// throws on a non-zero git exit, so each call-site swallows).\n    /// </summary>\n    private void EndOfRunSweep(RunContext context, IntegrationHandle? integ)\n    {\n        if (_worktreeProvider is not { } provider || integ is null)\n        {\n            return;\n        }\n\n        // Snapshot under the gate (no workers are running now, but keep the discipline consistent).\n        // Sweep only GREEN-owned directories; non-green tasks keep their worktree for fix/resume.\n        List<KeyValuePair<string, string>> sweepable;\n        lock (_gate)\n        {\n            sweepable = context.DirectoryOwner\n                .Where(kv => context.Settled.TryGetValue(kv.Value, out TaskResult? r) && r.IsGreen)\n                .ToList();\n        }\n\n        foreach ((string path, string owner) in sweepable)\n        {\n            try\n            {\n                provider.Discard(new WorktreeHandle { WorktreePath = path });\n            }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                _observer.CleanupFailed(owner, ex);\n            }\n            finally\n            {\n                lock (_gate)\n                {\n                    context.DirectoryOwner.Remove(path);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        try\n        {\n            provider.PruneOrphans(Array.Empty<string>(), integ);\n        }\n        catch (Exception ex)\n        {\n            _observer.CleanupFailed(\"(prune-orphans)\", ex);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private async Task WorkerLoopAsync(RunContext context, CancellationTokenSource runCts)\n    {\n        CancellationToken cancellationToken = runCts.Token;\n        try\n        {\n            await foreach (TaskEnvelope envelope in context.Channel.Reader.ReadAllAsync(cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false))\n            {\n                TaskNode task = envelope.Task;\n                WorktreeHandle handle = MaterializeForkIfDeferred(context, envelope);\n\n                if (CostCapHaltFor(task) is { } capped)\n                {\n                    await OnSettledAsync(context, task, capped, handle, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                    continue;\n                }\n\n                TaskResult result = await _executor.ExecuteAsync(task, handle, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                await OnSettledAsync(context, task, result, handle, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n            }\n        }\n        catch (OperationCanceledException)\n        {\n            // Cancelled drain.\n        }\n        catch (Exception ex)\n        {\n            lock (_gate)\n            {\n                _fault ??= ex;\n            }\n\n            context.Channel.Writer.TryComplete();\n            runCts.Cancel();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// plan 08 topology-wiring M1 §B: materialize a deferred fork-the-rest sibling's worktree at\n    /// dequeue — the actual <c>git worktree add</c> runs HERE, OFF the <see cref=\"_gate\"/> every\n    /// settling worker contends for. The fork roots off the producer's RECORDED sha (captured in\n    /// the request under <c>_gate</c> at assignment, W-2), never a live rev-parse of the segment\n    /// branch the inheritor may have advanced. Returns the envelope's existing handle unchanged\n    /// when there is no deferred fork.\n    /// </summary>\n    private WorktreeHandle MaterializeForkIfDeferred(RunContext context, TaskEnvelope envelope)\n    {\n        if (envelope.Fork is not { } fork || _worktreeProvider is not { } provider)\n        {\n            return envelope.Handle;\n        }\n\n        // git I/O off the gate.\n        WorktreeHandle handle = provider.ForkFromTip(fork.ProducerRecordedSha, envelope.Task.Id, attempt: 1);\n\n        // Bookkeeping under the gate: record the assigned handle + directory ownership.\n        lock (_gate)\n        {\n            context.Handles[envelope.Task.Id] = handle;\n            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(handle.WorktreePath))\n            {\n                context.DirectoryOwner[handle.WorktreePath] = envelope.Task.Id;\n            }\n        }\n\n        return handle;\n    }\n\n    private TaskResult? CostCapHaltFor(TaskNode task)\n    {\n        if (_plan.Config.MaxCostUsd is not { } cap || _journal.CurrentCostUsd() < cap)\n        {\n            return null;\n        }\n\n        return new TaskResult\n        {\n            TaskId = task.Id,\n            Outcome = TaskOutcome.NeedsHuman,\n            Summary = $\"cost cap reached: cumulative journaled cost has reached the configured \" +\n                      $\"maxCostUsd (${cap}); task not launched.\"\n        };\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Called after a task finishes (executor or cost-cap halt). For worktree-mode green results,\n    /// performs the B1 deferred settle (fragment merge → git integration commit → journal settle)\n    /// under <see cref=\"_integrationLock\"/> BEFORE updating the shared run context under\n    /// <see cref=\"_gate\"/>. This ordering ensures dependents only become ready after the upstream\n    /// integration has advanced the plan branch, making lazy handle creation FF-compatible.\n    /// </summary>\n    private async Task OnSettledAsync(\n        RunContext context, TaskNode task, TaskResult result, WorktreeHandle handle, CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        // B1 deferred settle (worktree mode, real segment): ValidateFragmentForSettle sets\n        // DeferredSettle=true, meaning the Scheduler owns the fragment merge → git commit →\n        // journal settle sequence under the integration lock.\n        //\n        // Old path (serial mode or fake provider): the executor already merged + journaled;\n        // just call provider.Integrate directly so IWorktreeProvider.IntegrateCallCount tests pass.\n        if (result.IsGreen && _worktreeProvider is { } provider && context.Integ is { } integ)\n        {\n            if (result.DeferredSettle)\n            {\n                await _integrationLock.WaitAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                try\n                {\n                    result = await SettleAsync(task, result, handle, provider, integ, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                }\n                finally\n                {\n                    _integrationLock.Release();\n                }\n            }\n            else if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(handle.WorktreePath))\n            {\n                // Non-deferred: executor already handled journal; just integrate the segment.\n                provider.Integrate(handle, integ, CancellationToken.None);\n            }\n\n            // #195 retry-salvage pruning (deliverable 6): once a task's FINAL settle (after the\n            // deferred B1 settle above, which can still turn a green-looking result into NeedsHuman on\n            // a failed union re-verify) is truly Succeeded, its salvage refs — its own prior\n            // rolled-back partial attempts — have served their purpose and are pruned so they never\n            // accumulate across a long-lived repo. Checked on result.Outcome (the POST-settle value),\n            // never result.IsGreen (which is true before SettleAsync can still flip it to NeedsHuman).\n            if (result.Outcome == TaskOutcome.Succeeded)\n            {\n                try { provider.PruneSalvageRefs(task.Id); }\n                catch (Exception ex) { _observer.CleanupFailed(task.Id, ex); }\n            }\n        }\n\n        var newlyReady = new List<TaskEnvelope>();\n        var newlyBlocked = new List<TaskResult>();\n\n        lock (_gate)\n        {\n            context.Settled[task.Id] = result;\n            context.Remaining--;\n\n            if (result.IsGreen)\n            {\n                // Which dependents of this producer just had their LAST pending dep cleared?\n                // (A multi-producer dependent here is a fan-in: it has other, already-green\n                // producers, and reaches the merged plan tip — never reused, M1 §A1.)\n                var justReady = new List<string>();\n                foreach (string dependent in context.Graph.DependentsOf(task.Id))\n                {\n                    if (!context.Settled.ContainsKey(dependent) && --context.PendingDeps[dependent] == 0)\n                    {\n                        justReady.Add(dependent);\n                    }\n                }\n\n                AssignDependentHandles(context, task, justReady, newlyReady);\n            }\n            else if (result.Outcome != TaskOutcome.Cancelled)\n            {\n                foreach (string dependent in context.Graph.TransitiveDependentsOf(task.Id)\n                             .OrderBy(d => d, StringComparer.Ordinal))\n                {\n                    if (context.Settled.ContainsKey(dependent))\n                    {\n                        continue;\n                    }\n\n                    var blocked = new TaskResult\n                    {\n                        TaskId = dependent,\n                        Outcome = TaskOutcome.Blocked,\n                        Summary = $\"blocked: dependency '{task.Id}' did not succeed\"\n                    };\n                    context.Settled[dependent] = blocked;\n                    context.Remaining--;\n                    _journal.MarkBlocked(dependent);\n                    newlyBlocked.Add(blocked);\n                }\n\n                // M2 §C / open-risk #4: a permanently-failed (needs-human/failed) task's segment is\n                // NOT Discarded mid-run. The \"fix, don't restart\" invariant (§3.2) keeps a failed\n                // attempt's worktree alive so a human (or a retry) can inspect the scoped-revert\n                // artifacts and WIP. Its directory stays owned in DirectoryOwner and is reclaimed by\n                // the end-of-run sweep at quiescence — which is what closes #126 (design §D point 2:\n                // \"#126 is closed by the run-end sweep alone\"). Cancellation skips the sweep, so a\n                // cancelled task's worktree survives for the resume prune (T-11).\n            }\n\n            if (context.Remaining == 0)\n            {\n                context.Channel.Writer.TryComplete();\n            }\n        }\n\n        _observer.TaskFinished(result);\n        foreach (TaskResult blocked in newlyBlocked)\n        {\n            _observer.TaskFinished(blocked);\n        }\n\n        // Each envelope already carries its assigned handle (fresh segment / reused directory) OR a\n        // deferred fork request the worker materializes off-gate at dequeue (M1 §B).\n        foreach (TaskEnvelope ready in newlyReady)\n        {\n            context.Channel.Writer.TryWrite(ready);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// plan 08 topology-wiring M1 §A/§B: assign worktree handles to the dependents of a just-settled\n    /// green producer <paramref name=\"producer\"/>, choosing reuse vs fork vs fresh-segment.\n    /// <list type=\"bullet\">\n    ///   <item><b>Multi-producer dependents (fan-in)</b> get a fresh <see cref=\"IWorktreeProvider.CreateSegment\"/>\n    ///     off the plan-branch tip, which already contains every producer's integrated work — never\n    ///     reused (§A1).</item>\n    ///   <item><b>Single-producer dependents</b> are the inherit-one/fork-rest fan-out. The inheritor\n    ///     (longest downstream chain via <see cref=\"DependencyGraph.TransitiveDependentsOf\"/>, ordinal-id\n    ///     tiebreak) reuses the producer's segment directory via the pure-handle\n    ///     <see cref=\"IWorktreeProvider.ReuseSegment\"/> (safe under <see cref=\"_gate\"/>; ownership\n    ///     transfers to the inheritor). The rest fork off the producer's RECORDED sha — a DEFERRED\n    ///     request the worker materializes off-gate (§B, W-2).</item>\n    /// </list>\n    /// Runs under <see cref=\"_gate\"/>. All assignment + bookkeeping is here; only the fork's\n    /// <c>git worktree add</c> is deferred off-gate.\n    /// </summary>\n    private void AssignDependentHandles(\n        RunContext context, TaskNode producer, List<string> justReady, List<TaskEnvelope> newlyReady)\n    {\n        // Fan-in (multi-producer) dependents reach the merged plan tip with a fresh segment.\n        var singleProducer = new List<string>();\n        foreach (string dependent in justReady)\n        {\n            if (context.ById[dependent].DependsOn.Count > 1)\n            {\n                WorktreeHandle fanInHandle = CreateFreshSegment(context, dependent);\n                context.Handles[dependent] = fanInHandle;\n                RecordOwnership(context, fanInHandle, dependent);\n                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], fanInHandle));\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                singleProducer.Add(dependent);\n            }\n        }\n\n        if (singleProducer.Count == 0)\n        {\n            return;\n        }\n\n        // Inherit-one: the single-producer dependent with the longest downstream chain reuses the\n        // producer's directory; ordinal-id tiebreak. The producer's handle carries the RecordedSha\n        // that Integrate captured during this settle (strict happens-before).\n        string inheritor = singleProducer\n            .OrderByDescending(d => context.Graph.TransitiveDependentsOf(d).Count)\n            .ThenBy(d => d, StringComparer.Ordinal)\n            .First();\n\n        WorktreeHandle? producerHandle =\n            _worktreeProvider != null ? context.Handles.GetValueOrDefault(producer.Id) : null;\n\n        foreach (string dependent in singleProducer)\n        {\n            if (dependent == inheritor && _worktreeProvider is { } reuseProvider && producerHandle is { } ph)\n            {\n                // Pure handle rewrite — no git, safe under _gate. Ownership of the producer's\n                // directory transfers to the inheritor.\n                WorktreeHandle reused = reuseProvider.ReuseSegment(ph, dependent, attempt: 1);\n                context.Handles[dependent] = reused;\n                if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(reused.WorktreePath))\n                {\n                    context.DirectoryOwner[reused.WorktreePath] = dependent;\n                }\n                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], reused));\n            }\n            else if (_worktreeProvider is not null && producerHandle is { } pf)\n            {\n                // Fork-the-rest: defer the git worktree add to the worker (off-gate). Root off the\n                // producer's RECORDED sha — never the live segment-branch tip the inheritor advances.\n                var fork = new ForkRequest(pf.RecordedCommitSha);\n                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], new WorktreeHandle(), fork));\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                // No provider (serial/fake-less mode): an empty placeholder handle, as before.\n                var placeholder = new WorktreeHandle();\n                context.Handles[dependent] = placeholder;\n                newlyReady.Add(new TaskEnvelope(context.ById[dependent], placeholder));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>Create a fresh segment off the plan-branch tip (or an empty handle without a provider).</summary>\n    private WorktreeHandle CreateFreshSegment(RunContext context, string taskId) =>\n        _worktreeProvider != null && context.Integ != null\n            ? _worktreeProvider.CreateSegment(taskId, attempt: 1, context.Integ, CancellationToken.None)\n            : new WorktreeHandle();\n\n    /// <summary>Record directory ownership for a non-empty handle path (M0 bookkeeping; under <see cref=\"_gate\"/>).</summary>\n    private static void RecordOwnership(RunContext context, WorktreeHandle handle, string taskId)\n    {\n        if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(handle.WorktreePath))\n        {\n            context.DirectoryOwner[handle.WorktreePath] = taskId;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// B1 fixed-order settle under <see cref=\"_integrationLock\"/>:\n    /// (1) deep-merge fragment into state.json,\n    /// (2) git integration commit (FF or non-FF merge),\n    /// (3) reserve mergeSequence + journal RecordSettle.\n    /// On non-FF failure: restore state.json, reset integration worktree, journal NeedsHuman.\n    /// </summary>\n    private async Task<TaskResult> SettleAsync(\n        TaskNode task,\n        TaskResult result,\n        WorktreeHandle handle,\n        IWorktreeProvider provider,\n        IntegrationHandle integ,\n        CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        string statePath = Path.Combine(_plan.PlanDirectory, \"state\", \"state.json\");\n        string preMergeState = File.Exists(statePath) ? File.ReadAllText(statePath) : \"{}\";\n\n        // B1 step 1: merge fragment into state.json BEFORE the git commit.\n        if (result.FragmentPath is { } fp && File.Exists(fp))\n        {\n            MergeFragmentIntoState(statePath, preMergeState, fp);\n        }\n\n        // B1 step 2: git integration commit (FF or non-FF union).\n        IntegrationResult integResult = provider.Integrate(handle, integ, ct);\n\n        if (integResult == IntegrationResult.FastForward)\n        {\n            // FF is free — no re-verify needed. Consume one merge sequence.\n            long seq = _journal.ReserveMergeSequence();\n            RecordSucceededSettle(task, result, seq);\n            return result;\n        }\n\n        if (integResult == IntegrationResult.Conflict)\n        {\n            // AI merge worker resolves the conflict (§9.1). If no worker is wired or all\n            // attempts fail, escalate to needs-human with a full B1 rollback.\n            bool aiResolved = _aiMergeWorker != null\n                && await _aiMergeWorker.TryResolveAsync(\n                    integ.IntegrationWorktreePath,\n                    handle.SegmentBranchName,\n                    _plan.PlanDirectory,\n                    ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n            if (!aiResolved)\n            {\n                AtomicFile.WriteAllText(statePath, preMergeState);\n                provider.RollbackMerge(integ, ct);\n                _journal.RecordSettle(task.Id, JournalTaskStatus.NeedsHuman, null);\n                return new TaskResult\n                {\n                    TaskId = task.Id,\n                    Outcome = TaskOutcome.NeedsHuman,\n                    ActionExitCode = result.ActionExitCode,\n                    Guardrails = result.Guardrails,\n                    Summary = \"merge conflict could not be AI-resolved; needs human\"\n                };\n            }\n\n            // AI merge succeeded: re-verify the merged bytes against the SAME integration\n            // set as the non-AI-merge union path below (§4.3, v1 contract). Running the FULL\n            // per-task set here false-fails by construction — it includes per-attempt\n            // anti-tautology guardrails (tests-fail-on-current-code, which PASS post-merge),\n            // scaffold-state and state-fragment-present checks (no action fragment exists at a\n            // union point), and downstream tasks that have not run yet. The B-3 \"AI may drop a\n            // colliding sibling's hunk\" concern is covered by the integration-scope union\n            // guardrails (a well-authored integration/union-verify guardrail catches a dropped\n            // hunk), the disjoint-scope CHECK, and the terminal integration gate — not by\n            // re-running the full per-task set (which would be inconsistent with the union path).\n            IReadOnlyList<GuardrailDefinition> aiIntegGuardrails =\n                GuardrailScopeFilter.IntegrationSet(_plan.Tasks.SelectMany(t => t.Guardrails));\n\n            ReVerifyResult aiReVerify = _reVerifier != null\n                ? await _reVerifier.ReVerifyAsync(integ.IntegrationWorktreePath, aiIntegGuardrails, ct).ConfigureAwait(false)\n                : new ReVerifyResult { Passed = true };\n\n            if (aiReVerify.Passed)\n            {\n                // B2: commit the AI-resolved staged merge with the task trailer BEFORE journaling.\n                provider.CommitStagedMerge(integ, task.Id, ct);\n                long seq = _journal.ReserveMergeSequence();\n                RecordSucceededSettle(task, result, seq);\n                return result;\n            }\n\n            // Re-verify failed after AI merge: B1 four-effect rollback.\n            // #188: persist the failing integration guardrails' output + a feedback.md to the task log\n            // dir BEFORE the rollback discards the merged bytes, so a human has the WHY on disk.\n            string aiFeedbackPath = PersistUnionReVerifyFailure(task, result, integ, aiReVerify, aiMerge: true);\n            AtomicFile.WriteAllText(statePath, preMergeState);\n            provider.RollbackMerge(integ, ct);\n            _journal.RecordSettle(task.Id, JournalTaskStatus.NeedsHuman, null);\n            return new TaskResult\n            {\n                TaskId = task.Id,\n                Outcome = TaskOutcome.NeedsHuman,\n                ActionExitCode = result.ActionExitCode,\n                Guardrails = result.Guardrails,\n                Summary = \"AI-merge resolution failed integration re-verify; needs human \" +\n                          $\"(see {aiFeedbackPath})\"\n            };\n        }\n\n        // Non-FF union: re-verify the merged bytes in the integration worktree.\n        IReadOnlyList<GuardrailDefinition> integGuardrails =\n            GuardrailScopeFilter.IntegrationSet(_plan.Tasks.SelectMany(t => t.Guardrails));\n\n        ReVerifyResult reVerify = _reVerifier != null\n            ? Restore state.json (undo fragment merge).\n        AtomicFile.WriteAllText(statePath, preMergeState);\n        // 2. Reset integration worktree to pre-merge HEAD.\n        provider.RollbackMerge(integ, ct);\n        // 3. Journal NeedsHuman — mergeSequence NOT consumed.\n        _journal.RecordSettle(task.Id, JournalTaskStatus.NeedsHuman, null);\n\n        return new TaskResult\n        {\n            TaskId = task.Id,\n            Outcome = TaskOutcome.NeedsHuman,\n            ActionExitCode = result.ActionExitCode,\n            Guardrails = result.Guardrails,\n            Summary = $\"non-FF union re-verify failed; rolled back (B1 four-effect) (see {feedbackPath})\"\n        };\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Persist a failed union re-verify's evidence to the task log dir (issue #188): one\n    /// <c>union-reverify-&lt;guardrail&gt;.stdout.log</c> per failing integration guardrail carrying its\n    /// captured output, plus the <c>feedback.md</c> the needs-human summary points at (which the B1\n    /// rollback path previously PROMISED but never wrote). Called BEFORE the rollback resets the\n    /// integration worktree, so the merged-bytes evidence survives the discard. Returns the absolute\n    /// <c>feedback.md</c> path for the summary. Best-effort: an IO failure here must never mask the\n    /// underlying re-verify failure, so it degrades to returning the intended path.\n    /// </summary>\n    private string PersistUnionReVerifyFailure(\n        TaskNode task, TaskResult result, IntegrationHandle integ, ReVerifyResult reVerify, bool aiMerge)\n    {\n        // The task log dir is the PARENT of this attempt's log dir. Derive it from the attempt's own\n        // relative logDir (logs/<runId>/<taskId>/attempt-N) — which uses the JOURNAL's runId, the same\n        // runId the executor writes attempt artifacts under — so the evidence lands beside them. Fall\n        // back to integ.RunId only if no attempt data threaded through (defensive).\n        string taskLogDir = result.PendingAttempt?.LogDir is { Length: > 0 } relLogDir\n            ? Path.GetDirectoryName(Path.Combine(\n                _plan.PlanDirectory, relLogDir.Replace('/', Path.DirectorySeparatorChar)))!\n            : Path.Combine(_plan.PlanDirectory, \"logs\", integ.RunId, task.Id);\n        string feedbackPath = Path.Combine(taskLogDir, \"feedback.md\");\n\n        try\n        {\n            Directory.CreateDirectory(taskLogDir);\n\n            var failingNames = new List<string>();\n            foreach (GuardrailResult failed in reVerify.FailedGuardrails)\n            {\n                failingNames.Add(failed.Name);\n                string safe = SanitizeGuardrailName(failed.Name);\n                // GuardrailResult.Output is the full captured output on failure (stdout, or stderr when\n                // stdout was empty); Reason is its first line. Persist both so the evidence is complete.\n                string body = failed.Output ?? failed.Reason ?? \"(no output captured)\";\n                AtomicFile.WriteAllText(\n                    Path.Combine(taskLogDir, $\"union-reverify-{safe}.stdout.log\"), body);\n            }\n\n            string mergeKind = aiMerge ? \"AI-merge resolution\" : \"non-FF union merge\";\n            string detail = reVerify.FailedGuardrails.Count == 0\n                ? \"The integration re-verify failed but reported no per-guardrail detail.\"\n                : string.Join(\"\\n\\n\", reVerify.FailedGuardrails.Select(g =>\n                    $\"## {g.Name}\\n\\n{g.Reason ?? \"(no reason)\"}\\n\\n\" +\n                    $\"Full output persisted to `union-reverify-{SanitizeGuardrailName(g.Name)}.stdout.log`.\"));\n\n            string feedback =\n                $\"# Task '{task.Id}' — union re-verify failed\\n\\n\" +\n                $\"Task: {task.Description}\\n\\n\" +\n                $\"The {mergeKind} produced bytes that FAILED the integration-guardrail re-verify, so the \" +\n                \"harness rolled the merge back (state.json restored, integration worktree reset) and settled \" +\n                \"this task `needs-human`. The merged bytes were discarded, but each failing integration \" +\n                \"guardrail's output was persisted next to this file:\\n\\n\" +\n                $\"{detail}\\n\\n\" +\n                \"This is typically a MERGE COLLISION (two colliding contributions combined into something \" +\n                \"that no longer builds/passes) — inspect the persisted output, fix the offending task(s), \" +\n                \"and re-run.\\n\";\n            AtomicFile.WriteAllText(feedbackPath, feedback);\n        }\n        catch\n        {\n            // Best-effort — never let a logging IO failure mask the re-verify failure itself.\n        }\n\n        return feedbackPath;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>Filename-safe form of a guardrail name for the #188 union-reverify log artifacts.</summary>\n    private static string SanitizeGuardrailName(string name)\n    {\n        Span<char> buffer = stackalloc char[name.Length];\n        for (int i = 0; i < name.Length; i++)\n        {\n            char c = name[i];\n            buffer[i] = char.IsLetterOrDigit(c) || c is '-' or '_' or '.' ? c : '_';\n        }\n\n        return new string(buffer);\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Record a worktree-mode SUCCESS settle (issue #196): journal a real\n    /// <see cref=\"Journal.AttemptRecord\"/> for the just-completed attempt TOGETHER with the reserved\n    /// <paramref name=\"mergeSequence\"/>, so a succeeded worktree task has a populated <c>Attempts</c>\n    /// list exactly like a succeeded serial task (SSOT §7). A result missing its <see cref=\"TaskResult.PendingAttempt\"/> (a\n    /// fake-provider path that never went through <c>ValidateFragmentForSettle</c>) falls back to the\n    /// attempt-less <see cref=\"ISchedulerJournal.RecordSettle\"/>, so no path regresses.\n    /// </summary>\n    private void RecordSucceededSettle(TaskNode task, TaskResult result, long mergeSequence)\n    {\n        if (result.PendingAttempt is not { } pending)\n        {\n            _journal.RecordSettle(task.Id, JournalTaskStatus.Succeeded, mergeSequence);\n            return;\n        }\n\n        var record = new Journal.AttemptRecord\n        {\n            Attempt = pending.Attempt,\n            StartedAt = pending.StartedAt,\n            EndedAt = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow,\n            ActionExitCode = pending.ActionExitCode,\n            Outcome = Journal.AttemptOutcome.Succeeded,\n            CostUsd = pending.CostUsd,\n            LogDir = pending.LogDir,\n            Provenance = pending.Provenance\n        };\n        _journal.RecordSettleWithAttempt(task.Id, record, JournalTaskStatus.Succeeded, mergeSequence);\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Shallow-merge <paramref name=\"fragmentPath\"/> into state.json at <paramref name=\"statePath\"/>.\n    /// The fragment was already validated (valid JSON object, no foreign keys). Uses atomic write.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void MergeFragmentIntoState(string statePath, string preMergeState, string fragmentPath)\n    {\n        var stateObj = (JsonNode.Parse(preMergeState) as JsonObject) ?? new JsonObject();\n        string rawFrag = File.ReadAllText(fragmentPath);\n        var fragObj = (JsonNode.Parse(rawFrag) as JsonObject) ?? new JsonObject();\n\n        foreach (var (key, value) in fragObj)\n        {\n            stateObj[key] = value?.DeepClone();\n        }\n\n        AtomicFile.WriteAllText(statePath, stateObj.ToJsonString(new JsonSerializerOptions { WriteIndented = true }));\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Convert an all-green report into a needs-human one when the terminal integration gate\n    /// (§3.3) failed on the final plan-branch HEAD. The failure is attributed to the\n    /// <c>integrationGate:true</c> sink task (or, absent one, the last task in plan order) so the\n    /// run is not certified and mergeOnSuccess is refused.\n    /// <para>\n    /// #175 attribution: a terminal-gate (typically whole-repo build/test) failure on the merged HEAD\n    /// is frequently a MERGE COLLISION — two tasks with OVERLAPPING <c>writeScope</c> on a shared file\n    /// both wrote new content there and git's 3-way merge silently kept both (a semantic duplicate, no\n    /// textual conflict marker). The harness cannot generically detect the duplicate (that is the build\n    /// guardrail's job), but it CAN name the suspects: the diagnosis lists every overlapping-writeScope\n    /// task pair and the shared path so a human immediately sees \"this looks like a merge collision\n    /// between task A and task B on &lt;file&gt;\" instead of a bare build error. Advisory and robust —\n    /// based PURELY on the writeScope-overlap structure (never the error text / a CS-code), and adds\n    /// nothing when no writeScopes overlap.\n    /// </para>\n    /// </summary>\n    private static RunReport WithTerminalGateFailure(\n        PlanDefinition plan, RunReport report, ReVerifyResult gate)\n    {\n        string gateTaskId =\n            plan.Tasks.LastOrDefault(t => t.IntegrationGate)?.Id\n            ?? plan.Tasks[^1].Id;\n\n        string failed = string.Join(\", \", gate.FailedGuardrails.Select(g => g.Name));\n        string summary = $\"terminal integration gate failed on final HEAD: {failed}\";\n\n        string? result)\n                ? result\n                : new TaskResult\n                {\n                    TaskId = task.Id,\n                    Outcome = TaskOutcome.Cancelled,\n                    Summary = \"not started (run cancelled)\"\n                });\n        }\n\n        return new RunReport { Tasks = results, Cancelled = cancelled };\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Per-task channel item pairing a task with its assigned worktree handle. When\n    /// <see cref=\"Fork\"/> is non-null the handle is a placeholder and the worker materializes the\n    /// real fork worktree off-gate at dequeue (M1 §B); otherwise <see cref=\"Handle\"/> is the final\n    /// assigned segment/reused directory.\n    /// </summary>\n    private readonly record struct TaskEnvelope(TaskNode Task, WorktreeHandle Handle, ForkRequest?  }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Executor with the new M1 signature (TaskNode + WorktreeHandle + CancellationToken).\n    /// Uses a rendezvous barrier: all N tasks must arrive simultaneously before any can\n    /// finish, which proves they overlapped. If the scheduler runs serially, the barrier\n    /// never opens and the test's deadline fires — a timeout, not a false-green.\n    /// </summary>\n    private sealed class BarrierExecutor : ITaskExecutor\n    {\n        private readonly int _expectedCount;\n        private readonly TaskCompletionSource _allArrived =\n            new(TaskCreationOptions.RunContinuationsAsynchronously);\n        private int _arrivedCount;\n\n        public ConcurrentBag<string> AssignedWorktreePaths { get; } = [];\n\n        public BarrierExecutor(int expectedCount) => _expectedCount = expectedCount;\n\n        public async Task<TaskResult> ExecuteAsync(\n            TaskNode task, WorktreeHandle worktree, CancellationToken ct)\n        {\n            Assert.NotNull(worktree);\n            AssignedWorktreePaths.Add(worktree.WorktreePath);\n\n            // Signal arrival; when all N arrive, open the rendezvous.\n            if (Interlocked.Increment(ref _arrivedCount) == _expectedCount)\n                _allArrived.TrySetResult();\n\n            // Block until ALL tasks are simultaneously in flight — this IS the overlap proof.\n            await _allArrived.Task.WaitAsync(ct);\n\n            return new TaskResult { TaskId = task.Id, Outcome = TaskOutcome.Succeeded, Summary = \"ok\" };\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static Scheduler Assert.NotEmpty(handle.PlanBranchHead);\n        Assert.Single(provider.CreatedSegments);\n    }\n\n    [Fact]\n    public void FakeWorktreeProvider_ReuseSegment_SetsTaskBaseToUpstreamRecordedSha()\n    {\n        // Encodes W-2: the reused segment's taskBase MUST be the upstream's RECORDED\n        // commit sha (captured at the moment the upstream committed) — never a live\n        // rev-parse of the segment branch, which the inherit-one successor may have\n        // advanced. A retry-reset to taskBase then discards only this task's WIP,\n        // never the upstream's committed work.\n        var provider = new FakeWorktreeProvider();\n        IntegrationHandle integ = Assert.Single(provider.ReusedSegments);\n    }\n\n    [Fact]\n    public void FakeWorktreeProvider_Integrate_IsNoOpAndCountable()\n    {\n        var provider = new FakeWorktreeProvider();\n Assert.Equal(1, provider.IntegrateCallCount);\n    }\n\n    [Fact]\n    public async Task Scheduler_DrivesThreeIndependentTasks_WithWorktreeHandles_OverlapProvenByBarrier()\n    {\n        // Three independent tasks at maxParallelism=3 with a rendezvous barrier.\n        // The barrier requires all 3 to arrive simultaneously before any finishes.\n        // If the scheduler runs tasks serially (regression), the 3rd never starts\n        // while the 1st is blocked, the barrier never opens, and the 30-second\n        // CancellationToken fires — making the regression a test timeout, not a\n The Scheduler calls\n        // IWorktreeProvider.Integrate CONCURRENTLY: the provider.Integrate(...) invocation in\n        // Scheduler.OnSettledAsync runs OUTSIDE the scheduler's _gate, so the moment the\n        // rendezvous barrier releases all 3 tasks at once, all 3 worker threads enter Integrate\n        // together. ## Task

**Scope boundary (harness-enforced):** Write only to
`tests/Guardrails.Core.Tests/WorktreeProviderSeamTests.cs`. After this task completes,
the harness runs a `git diff` check and rejects any edit outside this path — including
changes to production code (`src/Guardrails.Core/**`), other test files, or the
`.csproj`. An out-of-scope edit fails the task immediately and consumes a retry. If you
believe the fix genuinely requires a production-code change, do NOT make it — write
`{"needsHuman": "<why a production change is required>"}` to the state-out path and
stop instead.

### Background

`tests/Guardrails.Core.Tests/WorktreeProviderSeamTests.cs` contains
`Scheduler_DrivesThreeIndependentTasks_WithWorktreeHandles_OverlapProvenByBarrier`. It
proves the `Scheduler` genuinely runs 3 independent tasks **concurrently** (not
serially) by making all 3 rendezvous at a `TaskCompletionSource` barrier
(`BarrierExecutor` in the same file) before any of them can return — if the scheduler
regressed to serial execution, the barrier would never open and the test's own
30-second `CancellationTokenSource.CancelAfter` would fire, turning a scheduler
regression into an honest test timeout rather than a silent false-green.

This test is currently flaky **under load** (issue #214). CI evidence: on PR #213, one
of two identical parallel `macos-latest` jobs on the SAME commit failed with
`Assert.Equal() Failure: Expected: 3, Actual: 2` (on
`executor.AssignedWorktreePaths.Count`), the other parallel job on the same commit
passed, and a re-run of the failed job then passed. This reproduces locally too: 5
consecutive local runs of just this test produced 3 failures and 2 passes, with the
same `Expected: 3, Actual: <1 or 2>` shape — so this is a genuine, currently-live race,
not a one-off CI fluke.

### Your job

1. **Diagnose the actual root cause before changing anything.** Read
   `Scheduler.cs` (`src/Guardrails.Core/Execution/Scheduler.cs`) — specifically how
   worker tasks are started (`Task.Run(() => WorkerLoopAsync(...))`, one per worker, up
   to `maxParallelism`) and how each worker pulls from the channel and calls
   `_executor.ExecuteAsync(...)`. Plausible angles worth checking (confirm or rule out
   with evidence, don't just guess):
   - **Thread-pool starvation.** `Task.Run` schedules onto the shared .NET
     `ThreadPool`. If the pool's current thread count is below what's needed and the
     pool's growth algorithm doesn't inject a new thread fast enough (the CLR only
     grows the pool slowly past `ThreadPool.GetMinThreads()`, especially under
     concurrent CI load from other processes/tests), one or more of the 3 worker
     `Task.Run` continuations can sit queued for longer than expected before actually
     getting a thread — delaying when a task's `ExecuteAsync` runs and therefore when
     it can signal barrier arrival within the test's fixed window.
   - **The interaction between the per-task barrier-arrival signal and the
     `ConcurrentBag` write ordering** in `BarrierExecutor.ExecuteAsync` — check whether
     `AssignedWorktreePaths.Add(...)` and the `Interlocked.Increment` /
     `TaskCompletionSource.TrySetResult` sequencing could under-count arrivals, or
     whether the count assertions run against a bag that hasn't finished draining.
   - Anything else your investigation turns up. State in a code comment (near the
     fix) which cause you found evidence for and how you confirmed it — e.g. by
     instrumenting a temporary diagnostic run locally (do not leave temporary
     diagnostics in the committed test).

2. **Fix the actual root cause directly — do not paper over the symptom.** Explicitly
   forbidden: simply wrapping the test body in a retry-until-pass loop, and weakening
   any assertion to tolerate fewer than 3 arrivals (e.g. changing `Assert.Equal(3, …)`
   to `Assert.True(… >= 2)`). Either would defeat the test's entire purpose — proving
   genuine 3-way concurrency. Plausible legitimate fixes, depending on what your
   diagnosis finds:
   - If thread-pool starvation is the cause: force the pool to have enough minimum
     threads available BEFORE the test starts driving the scheduler (e.g.
     `ThreadPool.SetMinThreads` raised for the duration of the test, restored
     afterward, or a `Task.Factory.StartNew` with `TaskCreationOptions.LongRunning`
     equivalent applied consistently) — whatever addresses the actual delay you
     diagnosed.
   - If the timing budget itself is just too tight under load: widen the test's own
     margins (e.g. the 30-second `CancellationToken` deadline, or an internal
     rendezvous wait) — but only as PART of addressing the diagnosed cause, not as a
     substitute for it.
   - A more robust rendezvous mechanism in `BarrierExecutor` if the diagnosis points
     there instead.

3. **Do not weaken what the test proves.** After your change, the test must still:
   - assert all 3 tasks received distinct `WorktreeHandle`s
     (`AssignedWorktreePaths.Count == 3` and `.Distinct().Count() == 3`);
   - assert `Integrate` was called once per task (`IntegrateCallCount == 3`);
   - assert the overall run succeeded (`report.AllSucceeded`);
   - still fail (ideally via its own timeout, not a silent pass) if the scheduler ever
     regresses to serial execution — do not change `BarrierExecutor` in a way that lets
     it succeed without all 3 tasks genuinely overlapping.

4. **Stay inside your `writeScope`.** Only
   `tests/Guardrails.Core.Tests/WorktreeProviderSeamTests.cs` may change. Do not touch
   `Scheduler.cs` or any other production file — if your diagnosis genuinely requires a
   production-code change to fix correctly, stop and write
   `{"needsHuman": "<why>"}` instead of editing it.

### Verification you can run yourself before finishing

`dotnet test tests/Guardrails.Core.Tests/Guardrails.Core.Tests.csproj --filter "FullyQualifiedName~WorktreeProviderSeamTests" --nologo` — run it several times in a row
locally; it should pass consistently. This task's own guardrails will additionally run
it in a tight repeated loop and then the whole `Guardrails.Core.Tests` project once.

Conclusive across 13 runs: `assignedCount`=3 and `createdSegments`=3 **every time**, the barrier opens **every time** (huge thread headroom — starvation ruled out), but `integrateCalls` flakes across {1, 2, 3}.

**Root cause:** `FakeWorktreeProvider.Integrate` does a non-atomic `IntegrateCallCount++`, and the scheduler calls `Integrate` concurrently from all 3 worker threads — the `provider.Integrate(...)` in `OnSettledAsync` runs **outside** the scheduler's `_gate`. When the barrier releases all 3 workers at once, the three read-modify-write `++` operations lose updates → the counter reads 1 or 2, failing `Assert.Equal(3, provider.IntegrateCallCount)`. Both the local and macOS `Expected: 3, Actual: <1|2>` symptoms are this lost-update race (a `ConcurrentBag`-backed `AssignedWorktreePaths.Count` cannot under-count while `AllSucceeded` is true, which is why it's provably 3 every run).

Done. Here's the summary.

## Root cause (diagnosed with evidence, not guessed)

I reproduced the flake locally (9 failures / 13 runs), then instrumented the test with a **thread-safe** in-memory trace (my first attempt using `File.AppendAllText` was itself throwing `IOException` under concurrency and masking the real behavior — a good reminder that the diagnostic must not perturb the race).

The instrumented data across 13 runs was decisive:

| field | every run |
|---|---|
| `AssignedWorktreePaths.Count` (ConcurrentBag) | **3** — always |
| `CreatedSegments.Count` (serial `List.Add`) | **3** — always |
| barrier opened (all 3 arrive & overlap) | **yes** — always |
| available thread-pool threads | 32766 — **starvation ruled out** |
| **`IntegrateCallCount`** | **1 / 2 / 3 — flaky** |

The failing assertion is `Assert.Equal(3, provider.IntegrateCallCount)`. `FakeWorktreeProvider.Integrate` did a plain `IntegrateCallCount++`, and the scheduler calls `Integrate` **concurrently from all 3 worker threads** — the `provider.Integrate(...)` in `Scheduler.OnSettledAsync` runs *outside* the scheduler's `_gate`. When the rendezvous barrier releases all 3 tasks at once, the three non-atomic read-modify-write `++` operations lose updates → the counter settles at 1 or 2.

This also explains the CI symptom: a `ConcurrentBag`-backed `AssignedWorktreePaths.Count` **cannot** under-count while `AllSucceeded` is true (that assertion is only reached if all 3 genuinely executed), so the `Expected: 3, Actual: 2` was necessarily this same lost-update race on `IntegrateCallCount` — thread-pool starvation was not involved.

## Fix

In the in-scope file only (`tests/Guardrails.Core.Tests/WorktreeProviderSeamTests.cs`), made the counter atomic:
- `IntegrateCallCount` → backing `int _integrateCallCount` with `Interlocked.Increment` in `Integrate` and a `Volatile.Read` getter, with a comment documenting the confirmed root cause.

This fixes the actual data race — no retry loop, no weakened assertion. Every assertion is unchanged: all 3 distinct `WorktreeHandle`s, `IntegrateCallCount == 3`, `AllSucceeded`, and the barrier still forces genuine 3-way overlap (a serial regression would never open it and would fail via the 30s timeout).

## Verification
- Isolated barrier test: **10/10 pass** (was ~70% failure before).
- Full `Guardrails.Core.Tests` project: **730/730 pass, ×3 consecutive runs** (exercises the barrier under concurrent suite-load).

Temporary diagnostics were fully removed; the only repo change is the atomic-counter fix. Nothing to publish, so an empty `{}` fragment was written to the state-out path.
